# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad4695.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad4695.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Analog Devices Easy Drive Multiplexed SAR Analog to Digital Converters`. A family of similar multi-channel analog to digital converters with SPI bus. * https://www.analog.com/en/products/ad4695.html * https://www.analog.com/en/products/ad4696.html * https://www.analog.com/en/products/ad4697.html * https://www.analog.com/en/products/ad4698.html IIO ADC with per-channel child nodes. It exposes enum list covering 4 compatible token(s), including `adi,ad4695`, `adi,ad4696`, `adi,ad4697`, `adi,ad4698`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a enum list. Accepted compatible strings total 4: `adi,ad4695`, `adi,ad4696`, `adi,ad4697`, `adi,ad4698`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `spi-max-frequency`, `spi-cpol`, `spi-cpha`, `spi-rx-bus-width`, `avdd-supply`, `vio-supply`, `ldo-in-supply`, `vdd-supply`, `ref-supply`, `refin-supply`, `com-supply`, `adi,no-ref-current-limit`, `adi,no-ref-high-z`, `cnv-gpios`, `reset-gpios`, `pwms`, .... Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `avdd-supply`, `vio-supply`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `reg`, `spi-max-frequency`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`; supply rails `avdd-supply`, `com-supply`, `ldo-in-supply`, `ref-supply`, `refin-supply`, `vdd-supply`, `vio-supply`; GPIO controls `cnv-gpios`, `reset-gpios`. Pattern properties are `^in(?:[13579]|1[135])-supply$`, `^channel@[0-9a-f]$`. Child-node contracts include `^in(?:[13579]|1[135])-supply$`; `^channel@[0-9a-f]$` with child properties `reg`, `common-mode-channel`, `adi,no-high-z` and required `reg`; refs `adc.yaml`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Michael Hennerich <Michael.Hennerich@analog.com>, Nuno Sá <nuno.sa@analog.com>. Schema dependencies/references: `/schemas/spi/spi-peripheral-props.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `adc.yaml`. Conditionals/composition: `allOf`, `if`, `then`, `oneOf`. Examples present: 1. Integration points are `make dt_binding_check`, `make dtbs_check`, example extraction, in-tree DTS/overlay files using the compatible strings, generated `dt-bindings` headers where referenced by descriptions, and the matching IIO ADC drivers under `drivers/iio/adc/` or related MFD/platform driver glue.

## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file uses `unevaluatedProperties: false` after composed schemas. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. Conditional blocks require representative DTS coverage because a small `if`/`then` edit can reject one compatible variant while leaving others apparently valid.

## Test Signals
Useful test signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad4695.yaml`, repository-wide `make dtbs_check`, and building representative DTS files that use the listed compatible strings. For schemas with examples, successful example extraction and validation is the first regression signal. Driver-facing validation should also check probe logs for the relevant ADC driver, regulator/GPIO/interrupt acquisition, IIO channel enumeration, and any per-channel scale, offset, oversampling, differential, or calibration attributes implied by the binding.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad4695.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad7173.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad7173.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Analog Devices AD7173 ADC`. Analog Devices AD717x ADC's: The AD717x family offer a complete integrated Sigma-Delta ADC solution which can be used in high precision, low noise single channel applications (Life Science measurements) or higher speed multiplexed applications (Factory Automation PLC Input modules). The Sigma-Delta ADC is intended primarily for measurement of signals close to DC but also delivers outstanding performance with input bandwidths out to ~10kHz. Analog Devices AD411x ADC's: The AD411X family encompasses a series of... IIO ADC with per-channel child nodes. It exposes enum list covering 13 compatible token(s), including `adi,ad4111`, `adi,ad4112`, `adi,ad4113`, `adi,ad4114`, `adi,ad4115`, `adi,ad4116`, `adi,ad7172-2`, `adi,ad7172-4`, and others. Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`. Pattern properties are `^channel@[0-9a-f]$`. Child-node contracts include `^channel@[0-9a-f]$` with child properties `reg`, `diff-channels`, `single-channel`, `common-mode-channel`, `adi,reference-select`, `adi,current-channel` and required `reg`; refs `adc.yaml`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad7191.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad7191.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Analog Devices AD7191 ADC`. Bindings for the Analog Devices AD7191 ADC device. Datasheet can be found here: https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/AD7191.pdf The device's PDOWN pin must be connected to the SPI controller's chip select pin. SPI-connected IIO ADC binding. It exposes enum list covering 1 compatible token(s), including `adi,ad7191`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a enum list. Accepted compatible strings total 1: `adi,ad7191`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `spi-cpol`, `spi-cpha`, `clocks`, `interrupts`, `avdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `vref-supply`, `odr-gpios`, `adi,odr-value`, `pga-gpios`, `adi,pga-value`, `temp-gpios`, `chan-gpios`. Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `avdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `vref-supply`, `spi-cpol`, `spi-cpha`, `temp-gpios`, `chan-gpios`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `reg`, `clocks`, `interrupts`, `vref-supply`; supply rails `avdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `vref-supply`; GPIO controls `chan-gpios`, `odr-gpios`, `pga-gpios`, `temp-gpios`. No patternProperties are declared. No child-node pattern schema is declared; validation is focused on the ADC node properties themselves.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Alisa-Dariana Roman <alisa.roman@analog.com>. Schema dependencies/references: `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/spi/spi-peripheral-props.yaml#`. Conditionals/composition: `allOf`, `oneOf`. Examples present: 1. Integration points are `make dt_binding_check`, `make dtbs_check`, example extraction, in-tree DTS/overlay files using the compatible strings, generated `dt-bindings` headers where referenced by descriptions, and the matching IIO ADC drivers under `drivers/iio/adc/` or related MFD/platform driver glue.

## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file uses `unevaluatedProperties: false` after composed schemas. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. Conditional blocks require representative DTS coverage because a small `if`/`then` edit can reject one compatible variant while leaving others apparently valid.

## Test Signals
Useful test signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad7191.yaml`, repository-wide `make dtbs_check`, and building representative DTS files that use the listed compatible strings. For schemas with examples, successful example extraction and validation is the first regression signal. Driver-facing validation should also check probe logs for the relevant ADC driver, regulator/GPIO/interrupt acquisition, IIO channel enumeration, and any per-channel scale, offset, oversampling, differential, or calibration attributes implied by the binding.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. ## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file uses `unevaluatedProperties: false` after composed schemas. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. Conditional blocks require representative DTS coverage because a small `if`/`then` edit can reject one compatible variant while leaving others apparently valid.

Useful test signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad7380.yaml`, repository-wide `make dtbs_check`, and building representative DTS files that use the listed compatible strings. ## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad7405.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Analog Devices AD7405 family`. Analog Devices AD7405 is a high performance isolated ADC, 1-channel, 16-bit with a second-order Σ-Δ modulator that converts an analog input signal into a high speed, single-bit data stream. https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/ad7405.pdf https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/adum7701.pdf https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/adum7702.pdf https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/ADuM7703.pdf IIO ADC devicetree binding. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file uses `unevaluatedProperties: false` after composed schemas. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad7779.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad7779.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Analog Devices AD777X family 8-Channel, 24-Bit, Simultaneous Sampling ADCs`. The AD777X family consist of 8-channel, simultaneous sampling analog-to- digital converter (ADC). Eight full Σ-Δ ADCs are on-chip. The AD7771 provides an ultralow input current to allow direct sensor connection. Each input channel has a programmable gain stage allowing gains of 1, 2, 4, and 8 to map lower amplitude sensor outputs into the full-scale ADC input range, maximizing the dynamic range of the signal chain. https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/ad7770.pdf... SPI-connected IIO ADC binding. It exposes enum list covering 3 compatible token(s), including `adi,ad7770`, `adi,ad7771`, `adi,ad7779`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a enum list. Accepted compatible strings total 3: `adi,ad7770`, `adi,ad7771`, `adi,ad7779`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clocks`, `avdd1-supply`, `avdd2-supply`, `avdd4-supply`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `start-gpios`, `reset-gpios`, `io-backends`, `adi,num-lanes`. Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clocks`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`; supply rails `avdd1-supply`, `avdd2-supply`, `avdd4-supply`; GPIO controls `reset-gpios`, `start-gpios`. No patternProperties are declared. No child-node pattern schema is declared; validation is focused on the ADC node properties themselves.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. ## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

Specifications on the converters can be found at: AD7991, AD7995, AD7999: https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/AD7991_7995_7999.pdf AD7992: https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/AD7992.pdf AD7993, AD7994: https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/AD7993_7994.pdf AD7997, AD7998:... IIO ADC devicetree binding. It exposes enum list covering 8 compatible token(s), including `adi,ad7991`, `adi,ad7992`, `adi,ad7993`, `adi,ad7994`, `adi,ad7995`, `adi,ad7997`, `adi,ad7998`, `adi,ad7999`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a enum list. Accepted compatible strings total 8: `adi,ad7991`, `adi,ad7992`, `adi,ad7993`, `adi,ad7994`, `adi,ad7995`, `adi,ad7997`, `adi,ad7998`, `adi,ad7999`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `reg`, `interrupts`, `vref-supply`; supply rails `vcc-supply`, `vref-supply`. No patternProperties are declared. No child-node pattern schema is declared; validation is focused on the ADC node properties themselves.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

Maintainers: Michael Hennerich <Michael.Hennerich@analog.com>. Schema dependencies/references: dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Conditionals/composition: no explicit composition or conditional keywords. Examples present: 1. ## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. Useful test signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad799x.yaml`, repository-wide `make dtbs_check`, and building representative DTS files that use the listed compatible strings. For schemas with examples, successful example extraction and validation is the first regression signal. `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ad9467.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Analog Devices AD9467 and similar High-Speed ADCs`. The AD9467 and the parts similar with it, are high-speed analog-to-digital converters (ADCs), operating in the range of 100 to 500 mega samples per second (MSPS). `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,ade9000.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Analog Devices ADE9000 High Performance, Polyphase Energy Metering`. The ADE9000 is a highly accurate, fully integrated, multiphase energy and power quality monitoring device. Superior analog performance and a digital signal processing (DSP) core enable accurate energy monitoring over a wide dynamic range. An integrated high end reference ensures low drift over temperature with a combined drift of less than ±25 ppm/°C maximum for the entire channel including a programmable gain amplifier (PGA) and an analog-to-digital converter (ADC).... # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,axi-adc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/adi,axi-adc.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Analog Devices AXI ADC IP core`. Analog Devices Generic AXI ADC IP core for interfacing an ADC device with a high speed serial (JESD204B/C) or source synchronous parallel interface (LVDS/CMOS). Usually, some other interface type (i.e SPI) is used as a control interface for the actual ADC, while this IP core will interface to the data-lines of the ADC and handle the streaming of data into memory via DMA. In some cases, the AXI ADC interface is used to perform specialized operation to a particular ADC, e.g access the physical bus through... SPI-connected IIO ADC binding. It exposes enum list covering 4 compatible token(s), including `adi,axi-adc-10.0.a`, `adi,axi-ad408x`, `adi,axi-ad7606x`, `adi,axi-ad485x`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/allwinner,sun20i-d1-gpadc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/allwinner,sun20i-d1-gpadc.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Allwinner D1 General Purpose ADC`. IIO ADC with per-channel child nodes. It exposes oneOf with 2 accepted compatible shapes covering 2 compatible token(s), including `allwinner,sun20i-d1-gpadc`, `allwinner,sun50i-h616-gpadc`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a oneOf with 2 accepted compatible shapes. Accepted compatible strings total 2: `allwinner,sun20i-d1-gpadc`, `allwinner,sun50i-h616-gpadc`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `#io-channel-cells`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clocks`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `resets`. Required top-level properties are `#io-channel-cells`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `resets`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `#io-channel-cells`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clocks`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `resets`. Pattern properties are `^channel@[0-9a-f]+$`. Child-node contracts include `^channel@[0-9a-f]+$` with child properties `reg` and required `reg`; refs `adc.yaml`.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Maksim Kiselev <bigunclemax@gmail.com>. Schema dependencies/references: `adc.yaml`. Conditionals/composition: `oneOf`. Examples present: 1. Integration points are `make dt_binding_check`, `make dtbs_check`, example extraction, in-tree DTS/overlay files using the compatible strings, generated `dt-bindings` headers where referenced by descriptions, and the matching IIO ADC drivers under `drivers/iio/adc/` or related MFD/platform driver glue.

## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. Conditional blocks require representative DTS coverage because a small `if`/`then` edit can reject one compatible variant while leaving others apparently valid.

## Test Signals
Useful test signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/allwinner,sun20i-d1-gpadc.yaml`, repository-wide `make dtbs_check`, and building representative DTS files that use the listed compatible strings. For schemas with examples, successful example extraction and validation is the first regression signal. Driver-facing validation should also check probe logs for the relevant ADC driver, regulator/GPIO/interrupt acquisition, IIO channel enumeration, and any per-channel scale, offset, oversampling, differential, or calibration attributes implied by the binding.

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/aspeed,ast2600-adc.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `ADC that forms part of an ASPEED server management processor.`. • 10-bits resolution for 16 voltage channels. • The device split into two individual engine and each contains 8 voltage channels. • Channel scanning can be non-continuous. • Programmable ADC clock frequency. • Programmable upper and lower threshold for each channels. • Interrupt when larger or less than threshold for each channels. • Support hysteresis for each channels. • Built-in a compensating method. • Built-in a register to trim internal reference voltage. • Internal or External reference voltage. •... # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/atmel,sama9260-adc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/atmel,sama9260-adc.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `AT91 sama9260 and similar Analog to Digital Converter (ADC)`. IIO ADC devicetree binding. It exposes enum list covering 5 compatible token(s), including `atmel,at91sam9260-adc`, `atmel,at91sam9rl-adc`, `atmel,at91sam9g45-adc`, `atmel,at91sam9x5-adc`, `atmel,at91sama5d3-adc`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a enum list. Accepted compatible strings total 5: `atmel,at91sam9260-adc`, `atmel,at91sam9rl-adc`, `atmel,at91sam9g45-adc`, `atmel,at91sam9x5-adc`, `atmel,at91sama5d3-adc`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `atmel,adc-channels-used`, `atmel,adc-startup-time`, `atmel,adc-vref`, `atmel,adc-use-external-triggers`, `atmel,adc-use-res`, `atmel,adc-sleep-mode`, `atmel,adc-sample-hold-time`, `atmel,adc-ts-wires`, `atmel,adc-ts-pressure-threshold`, `#io-channel-cells`. Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `atmel,adc-channels-used`, `atmel,adc-startup-time`, `atmel,adc-vref`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `#io-channel-cells`. No patternProperties are declared. No child-node pattern schema is declared; validation is focused on the ADC node properties themselves.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Alexandre Belloni <alexandre.belloni@bootlin.com>. Schema dependencies/references: `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`. Conditionals/composition: no explicit composition or conditional keywords. Examples present: 1. Integration points are `make dt_binding_check`, `make dtbs_check`, example extraction, in-tree DTS/overlay files using the compatible strings, generated `dt-bindings` headers where referenced by descriptions, and the matching IIO ADC drivers under `drivers/iio/adc/` or related MFD/platform driver glue.

## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. Conditional blocks require representative DTS coverage because a small `if`/`then` edit can reject one compatible variant while leaving others apparently valid.

## Test Signals
Useful test signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/atmel,sama9260-adc.yaml`, repository-wide `make dtbs_check`, and building representative DTS files that use the listed compatible strings. For schemas with examples, successful example extraction and validation is the first regression signal. Driver-facing validation should also check probe logs for the relevant ADC driver, regulator/GPIO/interrupt acquisition, IIO channel enumeration, and any per-channel scale, offset, oversampling, differential, or calibration attributes implied by the binding.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/envelope-detector.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/envelope-detector.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `ADC envelope detector using a DAC and a comparator`. The DAC is used to find the peak level of an alternating voltage input signal by a binary search using the output of a comparator wired to an interrupt pin. Like so: _ | \ input +------>-------|+ \ | \ .-------. | }---. | | | / | | dac|-->--|- / | | | |_/ | | | | | | | | irq|------<-------' | | '-------' IIO ADC devicetree binding. It exposes single const covering 1 compatible token(s), including `axentia,tse850-envelope-detector`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a single const. Accepted compatible strings total 1: `axentia,tse850-envelope-detector`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `io-channels`, `io-channel-names`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`. Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `io-channels`, `io-channel-names`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`. No patternProperties are declared. No child-node pattern schema is declared; validation is focused on the ADC node properties themselves.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence

`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/lltc,ltc2496.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Linear Technology / Analog Devices LTC2496 ADC`. IIO ADC devicetree binding. It exposes enum list covering 1 compatible token(s), including `lltc,ltc2496`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a enum list. Accepted compatible strings total 1: `lltc,ltc2496`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `vref-supply`, `reg`. Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `vref-supply`, `reg`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `vref-supply`, `reg`; supply rails `vref-supply`. No patternProperties are declared. No child-node pattern schema is declared; validation is focused on the ADC node properties themselves.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/maxim,max34408.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/maxim,max34408.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Maxim MAX34408/MAX34409 current monitors with overcurrent control`. The MAX34408/MAX34409 are two- and four-channel current monitors that are configured and monitored with a standard I2C/SMBus serial interface. Each unidirectional current sensor offers precision high-side operation with a low full-scale sense voltage. The devices automatically sequence through two or four channels and collect the current-sense samples and average them to reduce the effect of impulse noise. The raw ADC samples are compared to user-programmable digital thresholds to indicate overcurrent... IIO ADC with per-channel child nodes. It exposes enum list covering 2 compatible token(s), including `maxim,max34408`, `maxim,max34409`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `reg`, `interrupts`; supply rails `vdd-supply`; GPIO controls `powerdown-gpios`, `powerdown-status-gpios`. Pattern properties are `^channel@[0-3]$`. Child-node contracts include `^channel@[0-3]$` with child properties `reg`, `maxim,rsense-val-micro-ohms` and required `reg`, `maxim,rsense-val-micro-ohms`; refs `adc.yaml`.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/mediatek,mt2701-auxadc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/mediatek,mt2701-auxadc.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Mediatek AUXADC - ADC on Mediatek mobile SoC (mt65xx/mt81xx/mt27xx)`. The Auxiliary Analog/Digital Converter (AUXADC) is an ADC found in some Mediatek SoCs which among other things measures the temperatures in the SoC. It can be used directly with register accesses, but it is also used by thermal controller which reads the temperatures from the AUXADC directly via its own bus interface. See mediatek-thermal bindings for the Thermal Controller which holds a phandle to the AUXADC. thermal sensor/ADC binding. It exposes oneOf with 4 accepted compatible shapes covering 15 compatible token(s), including `mediatek,mt2701-auxadc`, `mediatek,mt2712-auxadc`, `mediatek,mt6765-auxadc`, `mediatek,mt7622-auxadc`, `mediatek,mt7986-auxadc`, `mediatek,mt8173-auxadc`, `mediatek,mt7623-auxadc`, `mediatek,mt7981-auxadc`, and others. The Auxiliary Analog/Digital Converter (AUXADC) is an ADC found in some MediaTek PMICs, performing various PMIC related measurements such as battery and PMIC internal voltage regulators temperatures, accessory detection resistance (usually, for a 3.5mm audio jack) other than voltages for various PMIC internal components. thermal sensor/ADC binding. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Matteo Martelli <matteomartelli3@gmail.com>. Schema dependencies/references: dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Conditionals/composition: no explicit composition or conditional keywords. ## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/nuvoton,nct7201.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/nuvoton,nct7201.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Nuvoton nct7201 and similar ADCs`. The NCT7201/NCT7202 is a Nuvoton Hardware Monitor IC, contains up to 12 voltage monitoring channels, with SMBus interface, and up to 4 sets SMBus address selection by ADDR connection. It also provides ALERT# signal for event notification and reset input RSTIN# to recover it from a fault condition. NCT7201 contains 8 voltage monitor inputs (VIN1~VIN8). NCT7202 contains 12 voltage monitor inputs (VIN1~VIN12). power/energy monitoring ADC binding. It exposes enum list covering 2 compatible token(s), including `nuvoton,nct7201`, `nuvoton,nct7202`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/nxp,imx93-adc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/nxp,imx93-adc.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `NXP iMX93 ADC`. The ADC on iMX93 is a 8-channel 12-bit 1MS/s ADC with 4 channels connected to pins. it support normal and inject mode, include One-Shot and Scan (continuous) conversions. Programmable DMA enables for each channel Also this ADC contain alternate analog watchdog thresholds, select threshold through input ports. And also has Self-test logic and Software-initiated calibration. IIO ADC devicetree binding. It exposes oneOf with 2 accepted compatible shapes covering 3 compatible token(s), including `nxp,imx93-adc`, `nxp,imx94-adc`, `nxp,imx95-adc`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a oneOf with 2 accepted compatible shapes. Accepted compatible strings total 3: `nxp,imx93-adc`, `nxp,imx94-adc`, `nxp,imx95-adc`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `vref-supply`, `#io-channel-cells`. Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `vref-supply`, `#io-channel-cells`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `vref-supply`, `#io-channel-cells`; supply rails `vref-supply`. No patternProperties are declared. No child-node pattern schema is declared; validation is focused on the ADC node properties themselves.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Haibo Chen <haibo.chen@nxp.com>. Schema dependencies/references: dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Conditionals/composition: `oneOf`. Examples present: 1. Integration points are `make dt_binding_check`, `make dtbs_check`, example extraction, in-tree DTS/overlay files using the compatible strings, generated `dt-bindings` headers where referenced by descriptions, and the matching IIO ADC drivers under `drivers/iio/adc/` or related MFD/platform driver glue.

## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. Conditional blocks require representative DTS coverage because a small `if`/`then` edit can reject one compatible variant while leaving others apparently valid.

## Test Signals
Useful test signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/nxp,imx93-adc.yaml`, repository-wide `make dtbs_check`, and building representative DTS files that use the listed compatible strings. For schemas with examples, successful example extraction and validation is the first regression signal. Driver-facing validation should also check probe logs for the relevant ADC driver, regulator/GPIO/interrupt acquisition, IIO channel enumeration, and any per-channel scale, offset, oversampling, differential, or calibration attributes implied by the binding.

`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/nxp,s32g2-sar-adc.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `NXP Successive Approximation ADC`. The NXP SAR ADC provides fast and accurate analog-to-digital conversion using the Successive Approximation Register (SAR) method. It has 12-bit resolution with 8 input channels. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/qcom,spmi-adc5-gen3.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/qcom,spmi-adc5-gen3.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Qualcomm's SPMI PMIC ADC5 Gen3`. SPMI PMIC5 Gen3 voltage ADC (ADC) provides interface to clients to read voltage. It is a 16-bit sigma-delta ADC. It also performs the same thermal monitoring function as the existing ADC_TM devices. The interface is implemented on SDAM (Shared Direct Access Memory) peripherals on the master PMIC rather than a dedicated ADC peripheral. The number of PMIC SDAM peripherals allocated for ADC is not correlated with the PMIC used, it is programmed in FW (PBS) and is fixed per SOC, based on the SOC requirements. All... Qualcomm PMIC/SPMI ADC binding. It exposes single const covering 1 compatible token(s), including `qcom,spmi-adc5-gen3`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/qcom,spmi-iadc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/qcom,spmi-iadc.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Qualcomm's SPMI PMIC current ADC`. QPNP PMIC current ADC (IADC) provides interface to clients to read current. A 16 bit ADC is used for current measurements. IADC can measure the current through an external resistor (channel 1) or internal (built-in) resistor (channel 0). When using an external resistor it is to be described by qcom,external-resistor-micro-ohms property. Qualcomm PMIC/SPMI ADC binding. It exposes ordered items sequence covering 3 compatible token(s), including `qcom,pm8226-iadc`, `qcom,pm8941-iadc`, `qcom,spmi-iadc`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a ordered items sequence. Accepted compatible strings total 3: `qcom,pm8226-iadc`, `qcom,pm8941-iadc`, `qcom,spmi-iadc`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `qcom,external-resistor-micro-ohms`, `interrupts`, `#io-channel-cells`. Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `reg`, `interrupts`, `#io-channel-cells`. No patternProperties are declared. No child-node pattern schema is declared; validation is focused on the ADC node properties themselves.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Jonathan Cameron <jic23@kernel.org>. Schema dependencies/references: dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Conditionals/composition: no explicit composition or conditional keywords. Examples present: 1. Integration points are `make dt_binding_check`, `make dtbs_check`, example extraction, in-tree DTS/overlay files using the compatible strings, generated `dt-bindings` headers where referenced by descriptions, and the matching IIO ADC drivers under `drivers/iio/adc/` or related MFD/platform driver glue.

## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. Conditional blocks require representative DTS coverage because a small `if`/`then` edit can reject one compatible variant while leaving others apparently valid.

## Test Signals
Useful test signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/qcom,spmi-iadc.yaml`, repository-wide `make dtbs_check`, and building representative DTS files that use the listed compatible strings. For schemas with examples, successful example extraction and validation is the first regression signal. Driver-facing validation should also check probe logs for the relevant ADC driver, regulator/GPIO/interrupt acquisition, IIO channel enumeration, and any per-channel scale, offset, oversampling, differential, or calibration attributes implied by the binding.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/qcom,spmi-iadc.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/renesas,rcar-gyroadc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/renesas,rcar-gyroadc.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Renesas R-Car GyroADC`. The GyroADC block is a reduced SPI block with up to 8 chipselect lines, which supports the SPI protocol of a selected few SPI ADCs. The SPI ADCs are sampled by the GyroADC block in a round-robin fashion and the result presented in the GyroADC registers. The ADC bindings should match with that of the devices connected to a full featured SPI bus. SPI-connected IIO ADC binding. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/renesas,rzg2l-adc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/renesas,rzg2l-adc.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `Renesas RZ/G2L ADC`. A/D Converter block is a successive approximation analog-to-digital converter with a 12-bit accuracy. Up to eight analog input channels can be selected. Conversions can be performed in single or repeat mode. Result of the ADC is stored in a 32-bit data register corresponding to each channel. IIO ADC with per-channel child nodes. It exposes oneOf with 2 accepted compatible shapes covering 5 compatible token(s), including `renesas,r9a07g043-adc`, `renesas,r9a07g044-adc`, `renesas,r9a07g054-adc`, `renesas,rzg2l-adc`, `renesas,r9a08g045-adc`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a oneOf with 2 accepted compatible shapes. Accepted compatible strings total 5: `renesas,r9a07g043-adc`, `renesas,r9a07g044-adc`, `renesas,r9a07g054-adc`, `renesas,rzg2l-adc`, `renesas,r9a08g045-adc`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `power-domains`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `#io-channel-cells`. Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `power-domains`, `resets`, `reset-names`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `#io-channel-cells`. Pattern properties are `^channel@[0-8]$`. Child-node contracts include `^channel@[0-8]$` with child properties `reg` and required `reg`; refs `adc.yaml`.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Lad Prabhakar <prabhakar.mahadev-lad.rj@bp.renesas.com>. Schema dependencies/references: `adc.yaml`. Conditionals/composition: `allOf`, `oneOf`, `if`, `then`. Examples present: 1. Integration points are `make dt_binding_check`, `make dtbs_check`, example extraction, in-tree DTS/overlay files using the compatible strings, generated `dt-bindings` headers where referenced by descriptions, and the matching IIO ADC drivers under `drivers/iio/adc/` or related MFD/platform driver glue.

## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. Conditional blocks require representative DTS coverage because a small `if`/`then` edit can reject one compatible variant while leaving others apparently valid.

## Test Signals
Useful test signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/renesas,rzg2l-adc.yaml`, repository-wide `make dtbs_check`, and building representative DTS files that use the listed compatible strings. For schemas with examples, successful example extraction and validation is the first regression signal. Driver-facing validation should also check probe logs for the relevant ADC driver, regulator/GPIO/interrupt acquisition, IIO channel enumeration, and any per-channel scale, offset, oversampling, differential, or calibration attributes implied by the binding.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/renesas,rzg2l-adc.yaml -->

The Renesas RZ/N1 ADC controller available in the Renesas RZ/N1 SoCs family can use up to two internal ADC cores (ADC1 and ADC2) those internal cores are handled through ADC controller virtual channels. IIO ADC devicetree binding. It exposes ordered items sequence covering 2 compatible token(s), including `renesas,r9a06g032-adc`, `renesas,rzn1-adc`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/richtek,rtq6056.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/richtek,rtq6056.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `RTQ6056 Bi-Directional Current and Power Monitor with 16-bit ADC`. The RTQ6056 is a high accuracy current-sense monitor with I2C and SMBus interface, and the device provides full information for system by reading out the loading current and power. The device monitors both of the drops across sense resistor and the BUS voltage, converts into the current in amperes, and power in watts through internal analog-to-digital converter ADC. The programmable calibration, adjustable conversion time, and averaging function are also built in for more design flexibility. Datasheet is... I2C-connected IIO ADC binding. It exposes oneOf with 2 accepted compatible shapes covering 3 compatible token(s), including `richtek,rtq6056`, `richtek,rtq6059`, `richtek,rtq6053`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `reg`, `#io-channel-cells`. No patternProperties are declared. No child-node pattern schema is declared; validation is focused on the ADC node properties themselves.

State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/rohm,bd79124.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/rohm,bd79124.yaml` defines the Linux devicetree binding titled `ROHM BD79124 ADC/GPO`. The ROHM BD79124 is a 12-bit, 8-channel, SAR ADC. The ADC supports an automatic measurement mode, with an alarm interrupt for out-of-window measurements. ADC input pins can be also configured as general purpose outputs. IIO ADC with per-channel child nodes. It exposes single const covering 1 compatible token(s), including `rohm,bd79124`. Its primary consumer is dt-schema validation for DTS nodes that instantiate the ADC hardware; kernel drivers consume the validated ABI through standard OF, SPI, I2C, platform, regulator, GPIO, interrupt, clock, reset, and IIO channel helpers.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
This is a schema contract rather than C code, so the important APIs are devicetree properties, compatible strings, referenced common schemas, and child-node layouts. The `compatible` property is a single const. Accepted compatible strings total 1: `rohm,bd79124`. Top-level properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`, `vdd-supply`, `iovdd-supply`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`. Required top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `iovdd-supply`, `vdd-supply`. Important resource contracts cover bus/resource keys `reg`, `interrupts`, `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`; supply rails `iovdd-supply`, `vdd-supply`. Pattern properties are `^channel@[0-7]+$`. Child-node contracts include `^channel@[0-7]+$` with child properties `reg` and required `reg`; refs `/schemas/iio/adc/adc.yaml#`.

## Control Flow, State, and Persistence
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation: dt-schema selects the binding by `$id`, `compatible`, node name, or a referencing schema, checks required resources, applies `$ref` common schemas, walks child channel objects when present, and then enforces `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties`. There is no executable state machine in this YAML file. Persistent behavior is the devicetree ABI: compatible ordering, register or chip-select cells, supply names, channel numbering, interrupt naming, GPIO polarity, reference-voltage modeling, and any per-channel calibration settings must remain stable for existing DTS files and drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
State and persistence are external to the document. The ADC driver stores runtime state in kernel structures after probing, while this binding persists only the static hardware description. Edits that rename properties, change required supplies, narrow channel ranges, or alter compatible fallbacks can break previously valid board descriptions even though the file itself has no mutable storage.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Matti Vaittinen <mazziesaccount@gmail.com>. Schema dependencies/references: `/schemas/iio/adc/adc.yaml#`. Conditionals/composition: no explicit composition or conditional keywords. Examples present: 1. Integration points are `make dt_binding_check`, `make dtbs_check`, example extraction, in-tree DTS/overlay files using the compatible strings, generated `dt-bindings` headers where referenced by descriptions, and the matching IIO ADC drivers under `drivers/iio/adc/` or related MFD/platform driver glue.

## Risks
Primary risks are devicetree ABI drift, mismatched driver expectations, and schema closure mistakes because this file rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. For ADC bindings, high-risk changes include tightening `reg` ranges without checking channel maps, making optional regulators mandatory, changing SPI mode or `spi-max-frequency`, altering interrupt order/name semantics, dropping channel child-node properties used by board DTS files, or forgetting that common `$ref` schemas may already evaluate properties before `unevaluatedProperties` is applied. Conditional blocks require representative DTS coverage because a small `if`/`then` edit can reject one compatible variant while leaving others apparently valid.

## Test Signals
Useful test signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/rohm,bd79124.yaml`, repository-wide `make dtbs_check`, and building representative DTS files that use the listed compatible strings. For schemas with examples, successful example extraction and validation is the first regression signal. Driver-facing validation should also check probe logs for the relevant ADC driver, regulator/GPIO/interrupt acquisition, IIO channel enumeration, and any per-channel scale, offset, oversampling, differential, or calibration attributes implied by the binding.
